USE - The printing ink is useful for automobile                ornaments (claimed), preferably automobile                instrument panel and door plate.    ADVANTAGE - The method: uses the hydrogen bond connection                network to generate hydrogen bond site dissociation                when the film layer is stressed and deformed, so as                to realize transformation from deformation energy                to heat energy, eliminate local stress                concentration and prevent molecular chain from                breaking; uses aminated polysilsesquioxane as                discrete rigid supporting unit; can construct                thixotropic framework by the rheology modified                filler restricts the resin to flow so as to ensure                that the film layer keeps the microcosmic depth of                the texture in the stretching state and inhibits                the whitening.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - High-ductility 3D texture water transfer                printing ink, involves 45-55 pts. wt. polyurethane                matrix resin having a hydroxy value of 25-35, 8-12                pts. wt. aminated polysilsesquioxane having an                amino functionality of 6-8, 3-6 pts. wt.                rheology-modified filler, and remaining amount of                organic solvent, where the amino groups on the                surface of the aminated polysilsesquioxane are                associated with carbamate groups in chain segments                of the polyurethane matrix resin through hydrogen                bonding to form a hydrogen bond cross-linking                network, the hydrogen bond cross-linking network                performs stress dissociation and displacement                reconstruction of hydrogen bond sites during stress                deformation of the high-ductility 3D texture water                transfer printing ink film layer for automobile                ornaments, and convert mechanical deformation                energy into molecular chain segment heat                dissipation energy and eliminate micro stress                concentration generated by local deformation, the                aminated polysilsesquioxane acts as a discrete                rigid supporting unit and is arranged within a                thixotropic spatial framework formed by the                rheology-modified filler, the flow of the                polyurethane matrix resin in the 3D texture                vertical plane is restricted under a stretching                ratio of not less than 200%, maintain the stretched                texture at a microscopic depth of not less than 90%                of the initial state in the cured film layer formed                by the high-ductility 3D texture water transfer                printing ink for automobile ornaments.    An INDEPENDENT CLAIM is also included for                preparing high-ductility 3D texture water transfer                printing ink, comprising (a) adding polyurethane                matrix resin into a stirring container having a                heating function, heating to 45-50&#176;C, and                performing preliminary stirring at a rotating speed                of 500-600 revolutions/minute, (b) uniformly                dropwise adding aminated polysilsesquioxane into                the stirring container, where the mass ratio of the                aminated polysilsesquioxane to the polyurethane                matrix resin of 1:8.5-1:7.0, where the dropwise                addition time is set to 3-5 hours, and                simultaneously adding a rheology-modified filler                and an organic solvent, (c) increasing the rotating                speed of the stirring container to 3500-4500                revolutions/minute to perform a high-shear                anchoring treatment for 25-30 minutes, (d) online                monitoring the dynamic viscosity of the system, and                when the dynamic viscosity of the high-ductility 3D                texture water transfer printing ink for automobile                ornaments at 25&#176;C suddenly jumps from an                initial dynamic viscosity and stabilizing to a                final dynamic viscosity of 1850-2100 mPa.s, and                stopping shearing and cooling. The ultrasonic amplitude                changing mechanism is nested inside nozzle shell.                The ultrasonic energy exchange unit is installed on                ultrasonic amplitude changing mechanism and is                located inside nozzle shell. The feeding port is                located on nozzle shell. The discharge port is                located at nozzle shell. The feeding port is used                for making concrete enter ultrasonic amplitude                changing mechanism. The ultrasonic energy                conversion unit is used for receiving electric                signals and converting into mechanical vibration.                The ultrasonic amplitude changing mechanism is used                for amplifying amplitude of mechanical vibration                and acting amplified mechanical vibration on                concrete. The discharging port is used for                extruding and stacking concrete.    USE - Ultrasonic assisted 3D printing concrete                nozzle device.    ADVANTAGE - The device increases dimensional stability and                late strength of concrete material in printing                process, improves rheology and thixotropy, reduces                viscosity of printing material and optimizes                printing quality.    DESCRIPTION OF DRAWING(S) - The drawing shows a schematic view of                ultrasonic assisted 3D printing concrete nozzle                device. Length                of wire outlet holes and length of free jet section                is determined. Rheological parameter of molten silk                material is determined by using rheometer to test                to obtain rheology test data of molten silica.                Regression fitting to rheometry test data is                performed. Rheometric parameter and fitting                determination coefficient of rheogram model is                obtained by using the rheomer model with largest                fitting coefficient. Subsequent numerical                simulation is performed. Finite element model is                established. Parameterized geometric model of                nozzle wire outlet flow passage and free jet flow                section is established in the three-dimensional                modeling software according to the desired                cross-section geometry of the extruded wire.    USE - Design method for polygon-like wire outlet                hole of nozzle of FDM-three-dimensional                printer.    ADVANTAGE - The method effectively solves the problem that                the FDM-3D printing process, the printing product                has high roughness, large gap, improves the surface                and mechanical property of the 3D printing                product.  FR3105068-A1;  US2021187844-A1;  CA3103220-A1;  AU2020289803-A1;  FR3105068-B1;  NZ771326-A2	Three-dimensional printhead for three-dimensional            printer for printing on deposition surface using PVC            filament has printer nozzle and hot body, which are in            one piece	SERIN J;  JOUBERT P	RACCORDS & PLASTIQUES NICOLL (RACC-Non-standard);  RACCORDS & PLASTIQUES NICOLL (RACC-Non-standard);  RACCORDS & PLASTIQUES NICOLL SAS (RACC-Non-standard);  RACCORDS & PLASTIQUES NICOLL SAS (RACC-Non-standard)	202169725B	   NOVELTY - The print head (1) has a cold body (30) into                which a PVC filament in a solid state is fed,                provided for transmitting the PVC filament, still                in a solid state, to a hot body (10). The hot body                has a lower face (12) ending in a printer nozzle                (20) with an output orifice for the output of PVC                as extrudate. The printer nozzle and the hot body                are in one piece. The hot body is arranged to                enable radiation from the lower face in the                direction of the deposition surface, particularly                the hot body has an output heating element mounted                on or around the lower face of the hot body. The                output heating element is preferably selected from                among a resistor installed within the hot body, an                annular resistor attached to or embedded in the                lower face of the hot body, or a source of a jet of                hot air, provided to direct hot air to the lower                face of the hot body and/or the printing                surface.    USE - 3D printhead for a 3D printer (claimed) for 3D                printing on a deposition surface using a PVC                filament.    ADVANTAGE - The hot body and the nozzle are in one piece,                which provides greater precision in controlling the                temperature of the material at the output orifice                and prevents overheating the hot body, to achieve                an extrudate at an ideal temperature at the output                orifice. The nozzle is designed to reduce the                deposit of residues in the nozzle and the burning                due to material retention that can happen with PVC                material, thus, the nozzle is free from abrupt                changes in cross-section and has internal                geometries offering changes in cross-section                according to angles designed for PVC rheology,                which are less than 50 degrees. A heating element                on the lower face of the hot body allows to                uniformly diffuse the heat in the hot body while                putting distance between the heat source of the                resistor and the cold body. Providing a preheating                of the last layer deposited on the object being                printed, and/or the extrudate exiting the nozzle                re-softens the material and thus enables adhesion                of the new layer on the last layer deposited.  HERNANDEZ A M L;  KUPWADE-PATIL K;  LE ROUX A;  LE R A	ICON TECHNOLOGY INC (ICON-Non-standard);  ICON TECHNOLOGY INC (ICON-Non-standard);  ICON TECHNOLOGY INC (ICON-Non-standard)	2022C5334X	   NOVELTY - The nozzle (100) has a proximal end (102) and                a distal end (106) whose respective proximal                cross-section (104) and distal cross-section (108)                are different from each other.  SRINATH S;  KUPPUSAMY R R P	NAT INST TECHNOLOGY WARANGAL (NATE-Non-standard)	202626942F	   NOVELTY - Dual-cure resin hybrid nanocomposite comprises                10-25 wt.% ultra-violet(UV)-curable epoxy acrylate                oligomer, 1-10 wt.% glycidyl methacrylate                crosslinker, 1-4 wt.% phosphine-oxide                photoinitiator, 73-88 wt.% thermally curable resole                phenolic resin, 5 wt.% nanofillers comprise a nano                silica and 1-5 wt.% boron nitride nano powder,                direct ink writing (DIW) 3D printer with a nozzle,                UV light source. The UV-curable epoxy acrylate                oligomer, the glycidyl methacrylate crosslinker in                the phosphine-oxide photoinitiator and the                thermally curable resole phenolic resin are mixed                to form a reactive base resin. The nanofillers are                added into the reactive base resin under mechanical                stirring to form a nanocomposite ink.    USE - Dual-cure resin hybrid nanocomposite used for                additive manufacturing through direct ink writing                (DIW) 3D printing, for high performance thermal                shielding and protection, for aerospace and                high-temperature structural applications such as                aerospace thermal shields and nozzle liners and                other thermal-protection applications,                high-temperature shielding applications.    ADVANTAGE - The nano silica in functions as a rheology                modifier exhibiting enhanced shape retention during                printing and enhanced thermal-erosive resistance.                The boron nitride nano powder in enhances the                ablation resistance. The nanofillers in exhibit                thixotropic recovery enabling stable extrusion                without nozzle clogging. The UV curing provides                immediate green strength during layer deposition,                while thermal post-curing ensures full                crosslinking, high mechanical performance, and                strong interlayer adhesion. The optimized printing                parameters correspond to different filler loadings.                The nano silica improves rheology, shape retention,                and erosion resistance, whereas boron nitride                enhances ablation resistance. The composition                offers efficient, mold-free fabrication of                thermally resistant components. The composite                produces high-performance, thermally resistant                composite structures. The UV-curable components                provide green strength during printing, while                thermal curing completes crosslinking for high                mechanical performance. The optimized formulation                and printing method enable cost-effective,                high-performance printed composite structures. The                nanofillers exhibit thixotropic recovery enabling                stable extrusion without nozzle clogging. The                approach also supports cost-effective manufacturing                by removing the need for large-mold tooling and                lengthy thermal curing cycles. The printable                composite resin enhances mechanical performance.                The compressive strength of the dual-cure printable                resin hybrid composite thermal shields improves                with increasing boron nitride content in                formulations. The results highlight boron nitride's                critical role in enhancing the ablative and thermal                protection performance of the composite shields.                The dual-cure resin hybrid nanofilled composite                thermal shields for DIW printing are formulated                within carefully optimized parameter ranges to                ensure processability, in-situ stabilization, and                enhanced final performance. The optimized                conditions allow for smooth extrusion, reliable                layer stacking, in-situ structural stabilization,                and final composites with improved mechanical and                thermal performance, while offering tunability to                accommodate specific unit geometries and                performance requirements.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Dual-cure resin hybrid nanocomposite comprises                10-25 wt.% ultra-violet(UV)-curable epoxy acrylate                oligomer, 1-10 wt.% glycidyl methacrylate                crosslinker, 1-4 wt.% phosphine-oxide                photoinitiator, 73-88 wt.% thermally curable resole                phenolic resin, 5 wt.% nanofillers comprise a nano                silica and 1-5 wt.% boron nitride nano powder,                direct ink writing (DIW) 3D printer with a nozzle,                UV light source. The UV-curable epoxy acrylate                oligomer, the glycidyl methacrylate crosslinker in                the phosphine-oxide photoinitiator and the                thermally curable resole phenolic resin are mixed                to form a reactive base resin. The nanofillers are                added into the reactive base resin under mechanical                stirring to form a nanocomposite ink. The                nanocomposite ink is loaded into the direct ink                writing (DIW) 3D printer is equipped with the UV                light source, where the nanocomposite ink is                extruded through the nozzle to deposit layers while                exposing each layer to UV radiation to induce                in-situ gelation and green-strength formation to                form a printed structure and the printed structure                is thermally cured at 80-120 &#176;C in a                staged heating cycle to form a dual-cure resin                hybrid nanocomposite. The thermal post-curing                comprises sequential holding at 80 &#176;C, 100                &#176;C and 120 &#176;C for 2-4 hours to                complete phenolic crosslinking and to enhance                thermal stability.  PILLAY A;  GUERINI A;  ZAMPINI D	CEMEX INNOVATION HOLDING AG (CEME-Non-standard);  CEMEX SAB DE CV (CEME-Non-standard);  CEMEX SAB DE CV (CEME-Non-standard);  CEMEX SAB CV (CEME-Non-standard);  CEMEX SAB CV (CEME-Non-standard)	202530417U	   NOVELTY - Three-dimensional (3D) printing industrial                production for fresh construction material                containing a hydraulic binder, comprises: e.g. (a)                preparing a ready-mix construction material (CM) in                a concrete-type mixer or concrete batching plant or                local static concrete mixer/concrete lorry, from                the mix design of 250-700 kg/m3binder, 100-455                kg/m3water, (400-1100 kg/m3sand, and aggregates                having a size of 4-16 mm, preferably 4-12 mm,                (400-1200 kg/m3); (b) transporting the CM obtained                in step (a) to a jobsite equipped with a 3D                printing equipment; (c) optionally transferring CM                into a buffer container at the jobsite; (d) moving                the CM towards a printer head of the 3D printer;                (e) adding a first and/or second mixture system to                CM, uphill from a nozzle of the printer head of the                printing equipment yet at a distance located at                0.2-4 m from the nozzle, to obtain a second CM; and                (f) extruding second CM obtained in (e) through the                nozzle of printed head of printing equipment.    USE - The method is useful for 3D printing                industrial production for fresh CM containing a                hydraulic binder i.e. stiffened CM, which is used                to continuously produce rails, plates, barriers,                rods, bars, hollow pipes, roof tiles or the same,                or to deposit by 3D printing successive layers to                form walls, piles, pillars or the same (all                claimed), where the fresh CM is ready-mix concrete                or mortar containing water, binder, sand, small and                medium sized aggregates and various                additives.    ADVANTAGE - The method: is robust industrial process to                use CM prepared in ready-mix plants, presenting a                wide range of mix-designs with water to binder                weight ratio of 0.4-0.65 and transported to the                jobsites using conventional concrete transportation                lorries; utilizes special mixtures combinations to                transform a stable fluid concrete mix design into a                stiffened CM once it leaves the printer head to                ensure dimensional stability of the deposit                material, since the construction material must be                fluid yet very stable (absence of aggregates                segregation) during the transportation and during                the pumping to the printing head that can take many                hours; exhibits that the important stiffening                expressed by a yield shear value of the stiffened                CM for instance can be measured, using a shear                vane, thus showing the ability to maintain shape                after extrusion of the construction material after                adding first mixture system, is due to a chemical                strong interaction between second mixture system                and first mixture system, where the reaction in the                form a polymerization produces a 3D connected net                that immediately reduces the fluidity of the                construction by a matrix effect and provides                stiffness property that allow the extruded/printed                material to support its own weight without                deformation, keeping stable dimensions and                geometries of parts/layers from the nozzle/die;                enables to print construction materials that are                true concrete, with aggregates sizes up to 16 mm                /actually limited by the dimensions of the printing                nozzle as other tests have shown that aggregates up                to 32 mm can be used with no effect on the                properties of stiffened CM; avoids having silos                (cement, mineral additions, fine and coarse                aggregates, water, and mixtures) for mixing the                components of the construction material to be                printed on the job site, avoiding thus important                manpower, and time consuming refilling steps;                maintains all the advantages of CMs mixed in an                industrial concrete mixing plant, in terms of                mixing efficiency, quality, reliability and                constant quality of the delivered construction                material; enables to print important quantities of                CM per hour (depending though on the dimensions of                the printing nozzle) reaching up to 6 m3/hour i.e.                unparalleled in the industry today, this capability                is related to the very fast interaction between                second mixture system and first mixture system, and                to the fact that the process does not rely on                hardening/setting accelerators or rheology                modifiers, that have limited effect even when used                in combination; is very flexible, versatile and                robust compared to the previously known solutions                as the additions of first mixture system, second                mixture system and optionally third mixture system                and water reducing agent can be adapted to the                specifies of the industrial and jobsite contexts;                avoids the need of a dedicated mixer mounted on the                printer head in combination with the conveying                injection device, where an additional mixer mounted                on the printer head is typically required when set                accelerators are used or if the ingredients of the                material to be printed are mixed or remixed close                to the printing nozzle; can be used for any shape                that can be 3D printed (walls, containers, vessels,                profiles, pillars, slabs, hollow shapes, with a                wall thickness of 0.01-1.5 m (mono-wall or multiple                parallel walls); and can also be used for extruded                construction materials to produce profiles, tubes,                and rails, at industrial scale, by replacing the 3D                printing nozzle by an extrusion dies.  LIU Q;  ZHANG L;  ZHAO Z;  XU C	UNIV JILIN (UYJI-C)	202415029E	   NOVELTY - Manufacture of double network gradient gel                involves adding sodium alginate to deionized water                and raising the temperature to fully swell to form                a sol, after cooling, adding deionized water to                dilute it to obtain solution (1), adding                1-ethyl-3-(3-dimethylaminopropyl)carbodiimide                hydrochloride, N-hydroxy-succinimide and                N-(3-aminopropyl)methacrylamide to the solution                (1), magnetically stirring, washing, filtering,                vacuum drying, and grinding into powder to obtain                sodium alginate with double bonds after grafting,                adding N-isopropylacrylamide, N,N methylene                bisacrylamide and &#945;-ketoglutarate                particles to deionized water to prepare a hydrogel                precursor solution, adding sodium alginate and                carbomer with double bonds to the precursor                solution to adjust the rheology to obtain a slurry,                curing, placing in deionized water to swell,                placing the swollen sample on the anode plate in                the electric field of the pure aqueous solution,                and electrifying.    USE - Manufacture of double network gradient gel                used for three-dimensional printing material (all                claimed) for biomedicine and tissue engineering                applications.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Manufacture of double network gradient gel                involves adding sodium alginate to deionized water                and raising the temperature to fully swell to form                a sol, after cooling, adding deionized water to                dilute it to obtain solution (1), adding                1-ethyl-3-(3-dimethylaminopropyl)carbodiimide                hydrochloride, N-hydroxy-succinimide and                N-(3-aminopropyl)methacrylamide to the solution                (1), magnetically stirring under protective gas                atmosphere, washing the solution with ethanol and                acetone respectively, filtering and placing the                obtained solid into a vacuum drying oven to dry,                placing the obtained solid into a vertical                planetary ball mill and grinding into powder to                obtain sodium alginate with double bonds after                grafting, adding N-isopropylacrylamide, N,N                methylene bisacrylamide and                &#945;-ketoglutarate particles to deionized                water to prepare a hydrogel precursor solution,                adding sodium alginate and carbomer with double                bonds to the precursor solution to adjust the                rheology to obtain a slurry that can be directly                written into 3D printing, curing the printed sample                under UV light and placing in deionized water to                swell, placing the swollen sample on the anode                plate in the electric field of the pure aqueous                solution, and electrifying to form the electric                field to promote the metal ion of the anode plate                to move to obtain the metal ion and the modified                sodium alginate to form the alginic acid gel.    An INDEPENDENT CLAIM is included for the dual                network gradient gel, which comprises acid                derivative of formula (I). USE - Three-dimensional (3D) printable ink                composition used for architectural and structural                applications in construction (claimed), used for                sustainable building applications.    ADVANTAGE - The flowability may be further optimized                through incremental adjustments of water content or                organic additive proportions to achieve the desired                rheological behavior. The 3D-printable ink exhibits                a thermal conductivity in the range of                approximately 0.62-0.77 W/m.K, thereby providing                thermal insulation characteristics. The components                are combined with water in optimized ratios to                yield a homogeneous 3D-printable ink with                controlled rheological behavior and desirable                setting performance. The organic additives are                added to enhance their functional contribution to                the composition. The resulting composition exhibits                controlled rheology, flowability, and cohesion. The                composition demonstrates improved interlayer                adhesion, mechanical performance, and shape                retention, thereby providing a sustainable, 3D                printable ink capable of producing structurally                sound and durable printed elements for construction                applications. The jaggery is liquefied to                facilitate uniform integration into the                composition, enhancing plasticity and interparticle                bonding. The herada (Terminalia chebula or                Myrobalan) is processed into either a fine powder                or an aqueous extract to function as a natural                organic binder that promotes early setting and                densification of the matrix. The base matrix and                organic additives are combined in optimized                proportions with water to produce a homogeneous                3D-printable ink composition, with rheological                properties. The composition achieving enhanced                flowability, extrudability, shape retention, and                layer adhesion, which are critical for printing                complex and culturally significant architectural                patterns and structural components. The 3D                printable ink composition eliminates the necessity                for synthetic admixtures and commercial                viscosity-modifying agents (VMAs), offering an                eco-compatible, low-carbon. The 3D printable ink                composition facilitates controlled layer-wise                deposition, supports heritage restoration, and                meets sustainable construction objectives by                utilizing biodegradable, locally sourced, and                environmentally friendly materials. The 3D                printable ink composition demonstrates superior                fresh-state properties, with flowability in the                range of 155-169 mm, and build heights of up to 190                mm, while exhibiting thermal conductivity                characteristics suitable for passive insulation in                traditional-style wall systems. The 3D printable                ink composition may be composed entirely of                biodegradable, locally sourced materials that align                with circular economy principles and sustainable                construction practices. In some cases, the                biodegradable nature of the materials allows for                reduced environmental impact throughout the                material lifecycle, from sourcing through                end-of-life disposal. The locally sourced materials                may provide economic benefits by reducing                transportation costs and supporting regional                material supply chains. The 3D printable ink                composition by reducing permeability and creating a                denser microstructure through continued pozzolanic                reactions. The rice starch may improve internal                shear resistance of the 3D printable ink                composition and may enhance thixotropic behaviour                for maintaining shape stability during extrusion                and layer-wise deposition processes. The                composition is achieved by incremental adjustments                to either the water content or the proportions of                organic additives, fine-tuning enables the                composition to exhibit the desired rheological                properties suitable for extrusion-based additive                manufacturing, ensuring proper viscosity, cohesion,                and shape retention during the layer-by-layer                deposition process.The 3D printable ink composition                may exhibit specific performance characteristics                that enable effective 3D printing applications                while providing structural and thermal properties                suitable for construction and architectural                restoration projects. The 3D printable ink                composition may exhibit progressive strength gain                over weeks and months following initial placement,                with the organic additives contributing to early                strength development through enhanced particle                bonding and microstructural densification.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - An INDEPENDENT CLAIM is included for a method                for preparing 3D printable ink composition,                whichinvolves (i) screening the soil to eliminate                oversized particles and ensure uniform gradation,                (ii) blending hydrated lime and ground-granulated                blast furnace slag (GGBS) in 50% (w/w) and 50%                (w/w) of the total binder content respectively to                ensure pozzolanic activity, (iii) dissolving rice                starch in water to form a homogeneous slurry, (iV)                liquefying jaggery to facilitate integration, (v)                processing Herada into a fine powder or aqueous                extract, (vi) combining the base matrix and organic                additives in optimized proportions with                water. The high-energy                excitation liquid is a silicate solution, a                carbonate liquid, a concentrated ammonia liquid or                concentrated water.  YUE Y;  WU G	ELKEM SILICONES SHANGHAI CO LTD (ELRO-C)	202367934W	   NOVELTY - Additive manufacturing of a silicone elastomer                article from a two-part silicone composition,                comprises: (i) charging the first part of the                silicone composition into a liquid bath of a                three-dimensional (3D) printer; (ii) charging the                second part of the silicone composition into a                dispensing head of the 3D printer; (iii) dispensing                the second part of the silicone composition into                the first part of the silicone composition in the                liquid bath to form a first printed layer;  CN122057078-A	Carrying out multi-cellular bioprinting of            vascularized tissue constructs used in disease            modeling, and drug discovery, involves: preparing a            degradable matrix bioink comprising gelatin            methacrylate and gelatin, and sacrificial bioink            comprising hydrogel-forming component and calcium            agent	LIU J;  ZHONG R;  WANG W;  MAN K;  LU J;  WAN J;  LV J	UNIV HONG KONG (UYHK-C);  UNIV HONG KONG CITY (UYOO-C);  UNIV HONG KONG (UYHK-C);  UNIV HONG KONG CITY (UYOO-C)	202651636U	   NOVELTY - Carrying out multi-cellular bioprinting of                vascularized tissue constructs, involves: (a)                preparing a degradable matrix bioink comprising                gelatin methacrylate (GelMA) and gelatin; (b)                preparing a sacrificial bioink comprising a                hydrogel-forming component and a calcium agent; (c)                loading the matrix bioink and the sacrificial                bioink into separate cartridges of a multi-nozzle                three dimensional (3D) bioprinter; (d) co-printing                the matrix bioink and the sacrificial bioink in an                alternating or embedded pattern to form a 3D tissue                construct; (e) crosslinking the matrix bioink and                removing the sacrificial bioink to create                perfusable channels within the construct; and (f)                forming the vascularized tissue constructs                comprising a degradable matrix and an integrated                network of perfusable channels.    USE - Method for carrying out multi-cellular                bioprinting of vascularized tissue constructs used                in regenerative medicine, disease modeling, and                drug discovery.    ADVANTAGE - The method employs a dual-bioink strategy                involving a fibrous microgel matrix bioink with                tunable stiffness, self-healing rheology, and                microporosity to support capillary self-assembly,                and a sacrificial bioink that defines perfusable                macro-channels upon removal. The system integrates                top-down printing with bottom-up cellular                self-organization within a programmable bioprinting                platform. A key inventive aspect lies in                engineering the physical topography of the matrix                to actively guide vascular morphogenesis, moving                beyond conventional permissive scaffolds. The                integrated platform enables the fabrication of                complex, biomimetic vascular networks with enhanced                mass transport, cell viability, and in vivo                integration potential. The method also allow to                overcome the limitations of existing top-down and                bottom-up biofabrication methods, which have far                failed to provide an integrated platform capable of                generating both perfusable macrovascular channels                and biologically self-organized microvascular                networks within a single tissue construct. DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Preparing 3 dimensional ceramic block involves                dissolving ceramic precursor in an organic solvent                under stirring at 30-70&#176; C, then partially                volatilizing organic solvent to obtain slurry. The                slurry has 65-85 wt.% solid content, viscosity of                at a shear rate of 1 per second is 500-1000                Pascal/seconds. The ceramic precursor is an organic                polymer ceramic precursor, which has molecular                weight of 1000-2000 g/mol and a degree of                polymerization of 20-40. The organic solvent is                selected from n-hexane, cyclohexane, xylene,                tetrahydrofuran, or toluene. The slurry obtained is                subjected to degassing treatment and performing                direct writing forming process to directly print 3D                pattern design on the substrate to obtain                semi-finished product. The semi-finished product is                completely volatilized at room temperature and                placed in an oxygen-containing atmosphere and                subjected to preoxidation treatment at                200-400&#176; C for at least 1 hour and                pyrolyzed at 1000-1400&#176; C for 2 hours to                obtain 3 dimensional ceramic block. The 3                dimensional ceramic block is also prepared by                mixing ceramic precursor and cross-linking agent,                then loaded into the reaction device and subjected                to cross-linking reaction under closed condition at                100-150&#176; C for 4-8 hours to obtain slurry                with viscosity of less than 100 megapascal/second.                The mixing temperature is lowered and volatilized                to obtain 65-85 wt.% paste for printing, where                paste viscosity has shear rate in 1 per second is                500-1000 Pascal/second. The organic polymer ceramic                precursor has a molecular weight of 1000-2000 g/mol                and degree of polymerization of 20-40. The paste is                subjected to degassing treatment and performing                direct writing forming process to directly print 3D                pattern design on the substrate to obtain                semi-finished product. The semi-finished product is                completely volatilized at room temperature under                protective atmosphere, and pyrolyzed at                1200-1400&#176; C 2 hours to obtain 3                dimensional ceramic block.
